# 17 November 2026 — F45 unified product creation and configuration

**IMPLEMENTED AND LOCALLY VERIFIED; owner acceptance pending; NOT committed or pushed.** Development remains at b6efb22e470417a7f62d0a830770ddc7a9855898. Preserve all prior uncommitted F20 work and unrelated drafts. Add/Edit Product now groups Basic Information, Dimensions & Weight, Recipe / Raw Materials, and Eligible Production Lines & Rates in one existing-style responsive EN/AR dialog. Blank new code generates a unique PRD code server-side; manual code remains optional and factory uniqueness is retained. Blank existing code keeps its identifier. Category is optional.

Recipes reuse bom_versions/bom_items: an inline product-only recipe belongs to the finished product, without requiring an extra compound. Optionally save a reusable manufactured-intermediate recipe, or select an existing one with read-only ingredient inspection. No shared recipe is silently edited. Changes append versions, retain exact ingredient decimals, original batch basis and forecast allowance, and reuse existing material-source validation. New complete recipes default to DIRECT_RAW (compound manufacture for kg intermediates); preserve or explicitly choose an existing physical source and retain stage-specific routes. Incomplete configuration can be saved using existing product semantics; missing recipe/weight/source/capabilities still blocks production. No new draft-status or inventory model was added.

Line choices write only existing work_center_capabilities through the current protected writer, preserving other products, setup times, unassigned-machine capabilities and inherited cut-length records. Select real line machines with configured categories and enter a positive rate in the product production unit per hour. Multiple lines are supported; existing routing/dependency/stop/material checks still control actual execution. Do not infer physical capability or invent rates. Removing/changing participating active-work capabilities is blocked; adding an eligible alternative remains possible. No automatic Planning or execution transfer occurs.

Weight basis is explicit for new/changed measurements: kg per stock unit, or measured piece kg divided by its positive measured length when stock is meter. For piece stock, the measured piece weight is kg/piece; kg intermediates remain kg/kg. Thus 3 kg / 2 m = 1.5 kg/m. Derived weight is calculated authoritatively with SQL numeric arithmetic. Existing legacy weight/dimensions/unit_weight_kg are retained without inference or backfill; exact decimal strings prevent browser rounding during unchanged edits. Historical positive weights and cut identities cannot be rewritten here. Recipe/weight/unit edits cannot change active or partially produced work. Existing supported stock units are meter, piece and kg for manufactured intermediates; new units need a separate contract.

Verification: 13 focused Node checks; 50 rollback DB assertions (28 configuration, 11 actual production/consumption, 8 decimal/batch compatibility, 3 all-or-nothing rollback), 3 observed-lock concurrent-save races, TypeScript, EN browser create/edit and AR mobile/reusable-recipe save. Synthetic browser product retained its generated code, 1.5 kg/m derived weight, product-only BOM and two line rates; a second product reused the existing shared recipe. Actual recording consumed exactly 0.62 kg for 0.4 m at 1.5 kg/m plus 0.02 kg Scrap; retry did not consume twice. 100 kg recipe edits retain their batch basis and existing calculation. No full suite, optimized production build, hosted acceptance or broad performance campaign is claimed.

## Warehouse master catalogue and confirmed replenishment — 2026-10-03

This is a historical milestone. At that checkpoint it superseded older 66-migration/428-test counts and automatic Min/Max creation statements below. Current materials have no manufactured-source/Production mapping and remain externally supplied.
- **IMPLEMENTED — shared stock truth:** Available = On Hand − Reserved. FG Projected = Available + planned/active remaining Production − incoming allocations committed to Sales − uncovered approved Sales quantity. The old projected function delegates to one batched helper; every existing product/meter/piece matched the old calculation in rollback before application. Materials Projected = Available; Incoming is NULL because no material incoming-supply model exists. Minimum is low at Projected <= Min, including equality; NULL Min/Max is not zero.

## Optional Sales targets and inventory supply sources — 2026-10-03 (previous milestone)

This section supersedes historical current-count, checkpoint-only scope and next-task statements below. Continued the clean authoritative `development` checkout at `f5896f1e354554641768ea35f6a78d42f1bde17e`. This implementation remains **uncommitted**; HEAD, `main` and `qa-baseline-2026-09-29` are unchanged. No push, branch change or DEV connection. The completed Maintenance checkpoint remains intact.

- **IMPLEMENTED — delivery semantics:** Customer Requested Delivery is optional from native EN/AR form through authenticated API, RPC and nullable database column. Empty input stores NULL; no customer target means no fabricated backward target, urgency, deadline risk or feasibility verdict. Native input handling preserves factory-timezone values when another field changes. Sales no longer submits a promised-date field; RPC payloads reject it and a database trigger preserves historical promises unchanged. Historical values are displayed read only and never substituted for requested dates or calculated forecasts.
- **IMPLEMENTED — Planning guidance:** the existing drawer reads exact selected-item context, configured capacity/rate/setup/calendar and the existing safety buffer. It shows target, buffered ready target, working duration and suggested start/finish when calculable, achievable/tight/unachievable/insufficient-data state, and the proposed finish's signed margin and yes/no customer-target result. Find Slot remains advisory and requires normal Preview/Confirm. No automatic scheduling, cascading changes or new buffer policy. No-date jobs use normal forward suggestions.
- **IMPLEMENTED — readiness:** persisted `start_time`/`expected_finish` remain Planning truth. Estimated Ready uses saved allocated incoming finish, adjusts a still-future active estimate for an actual late start, and uses durable reservation/dispatch timestamps for fulfilled stock. Unscheduled/uncovered supply or overdue unknown completion stays NULL; earliest feasible is a separate capacity advisory. Actual execution never rewrites planned timestamps. Multi-product hold/complete-line release, Good-only completion, Scrap/WIP separation and physical Dispatch are preserved.
- **IMPLEMENTED — inventory:** reused typed FG balances, reservations, incoming allocations, immutable movements, structure locks, retry ledger and Audit. Opening Balance and External Receipt add positive physical stock without Production; opening is one-time before prior stock-affecting movements. FG future corrections use existing Adjust Stock and reason. Receipts record item/unit/source/reference/note, authenticated actor and server time. Movement history separates On Hand and Reserved deltas, with actor display name and historical unknown-unit labels.
- **IMPLEMENTED — sourcing:** products default to `MANUFACTURED`; `EXTERNAL` and `BOTH` are separate from MTO/MTS policy. Only MANUFACTURED automatically generates Sales shortage or MTS refill Production. EXTERNAL shows external replenishment; BOTH shows a sourcing decision. Deliberate Internal Production remains available for BOTH; a Good receipt or external receipt can reserve its uncovered Sales demand. Atomic source/stock configuration avoids transient manufacturing. Actual stock reserves normally regardless of source; existing incoming commitments are respected. EXTERNAL planned/active production is rejected at the database boundary.
- **IMPLEMENTED — materials:** existing `materials` and `inventory_transactions` represent external RAW_MATERIAL / SEMI_FINISHED inventory separately from FG and WIP. New material creation offers kg/meter/piece; existing configured legacy units remain receivable without conversion. Material On Hand sums the signed ledger, Reserved sums unreleased material reservations, Available subtracts Reserved. New movements retain the configured unit; older movements remain NULL-unit rather than invented historical units. No Purchasing, BOM consumption, accounting, costing or alternative raw-material ledger.
- **Permissions:** existing per-person Sales create/edit/approve and Warehouse view/edit/issue remain authoritative. View reads stock/movements, edit configures sources/materials and receives stock, issue dispatches. No new permission definitions, accounts or grants. - Commands reuse authenticated `/api/data`, explicit SQL permission checks, the existing structure lock and Audit trigger. Factory/machine/line/downtime composite references enforce isolation. - Lifecycle: OPEN → ASSIGNED → IN_PROGRESS → COMPLETED → VERIFIED, plus reasoned CANCELLED. Completion requires a work note and does not verify the repair or change the physical machine. Verification requires a note, management permission and a person different from both the current assignee and completer. RESOLVED records verifier/server time and closes VERIFIED; REWORK_REQUIRED returns IN_PROGRESS with review evidence retained in Audit. Terminal requests cannot be edited through ordinary commands.

## Sales Order → Inventory → Production → Delivery V1 — implemented and live-verified on TESTING, uncommitted

This section supersedes the earlier migration/test counts and next-task statements. Continues `development` at `c494df9f6556bc925ccf75219a528c161e956028`. No commit, push, branch change or DEV connection. The accepted Planning, execution, Recording, Shift, WIP, machine/transfer and Product-color work remains preserved.

- Sales Orders and Warehouse are real permission-controlled pages. Customer/reference is free text; orders contain unique finished Products with positive typed m/pcs quantities, an immutable requester/creator, requested date, independent authorized promised date, buffer and notes. Drafts have no reservation/production side effects. Approval explicitly reserves free physical FG, allocates eligible incoming work, then generates only the exact remaining shortage as a high-priority Sales Production request. Legacy requests keep nullable origin; none became invented Sales Orders.
- Existing inventory is material/reservation based; WIP retains its separate lots/movements/quantity-scoped operations. New FG balances, reservations, incoming allocations, movement/receipt ledgers and retry records provide finished-stock truth without historical Good backfill. Only new effective Good output creates FG; Scrap and WIP do not. Corrections cannot remove already committed/dispatched stock. Overproduction remains available finished stock. Available = On Hand − Reserved; Projected also includes remaining incoming minus its Sales commitments and uncovered approved Sales demand.
- Make-to-Order remains the default and does not auto-refill. Explicit Make-to-Stock configuration uses typed unit/Min/Max. When projected free stock is below Min, replenish toward Max with a distinct low-priority request; eligible existing incoming prevents duplicate production. Only editable unplanned/unstarted/unlocked/unallocated refill quantity may refresh automatically. Persisted Planning schedules never move automatically.
- Full-order hold is the default. An explicit early-release flag permits an entire ready Product line; no arbitrary partial dispatch quantity is accepted. Warehouse confirms actual physical departure through an application dialog showing the products/quantities; only then does On Hand/reservation decrease and dispatched quantity increase. Sales completes only after all quantities are dispatched. Sales cancellation also uses an explicit application confirmation, preserves its reason, releases commitments and never cancels an active job.
- Delivery buffer defaults to two days, with audited Settings/authorized order overrides. Requested and promised dates do not move with forecasts. Earliest feasible uses configured compatible rates/setup/calendar/free capacity; current-plan Estimated Ready separately uses persisted incoming schedules. Unknown/overdue active completion stays unknown. Backward Find Slot is advisory, finds a latest compatible gap before the buffered target, and still requires normal manual Preview/Confirm. Sales, Planning and Factory Floor expose linked demand type/due/target/risk without changing Product identity colors.
- Commands retain per-person factory authorization and serialize on the existing factory structure lock, with row locks and actor/operation/payload-bound request IDs. ## Unfinished Production / WIP V1 and History column filters — implemented, uncommitted

- Continues `development` at `a228d99221d9c6401ab676c0cb710f1ae5cb0628`. All legitimate pre-existing Shift Time Windows and History refresh work is preserved. - Unfinished output is physically usable material needing another operation, neither Good nor Scrap. It retains the same Product and originating Product Item/request. Existing inventory is material/reservation based; the existing routing-step schema has no implemented transformation caller or configured TESTING route. V1 adds only `unfinished_lots`, `unfinished_operations`, and `unfinished_movements`, reusing Production Recording and correction/audit conventions. Discovery considered Autodesk Fusion Operations rework traceability and MRPeasy production/WIP separation; no full warehouse, fake product, costing or BOM was added.
- Each Recording has original/effective Good, Scrap and Unfinished, plus required Remaining Work when Unfinished > 0. Legacy gross remains Good + Scrap. Only Good advances demand; unfinished-only output can create stock without fabricating gross. Old entries retain NULL new fields; no historical backfill or approved Production Loss/OEE metric change. History output sums describe declarations across operations, not unique finished stock or current available WIP.
- A lot owns its source entry, created/available quantity, remaining-work note, actor/server time. Joins supply product/item/request/unit/origin; no duplicate product identity. Creation, negative consumption, and correction movements retain entry/correction linkage. Fully consumed and zero-quantity adjusted lots remain auditable; available inventory excludes them by default. The page shows product, availability/unit, remaining work, source request/unit/time, a short lot reference, movement history, and Use in Production.
- The user's explicit secondary-line decision is implemented as a separate **quantity-scoped WIP operation assignment**: source lot + immutable quantity + remaining operation note + destination line OR independent work center + resulting entry + actor/time. Assignment and output recording happen atomically per declaration. Original Product Item line, status and planned/actual timestamps never move; no second ordinary active-line job, reservation, or WIP Start/Finish lifecycle is created. A compatible secondary line cannot be occupied by another normal active item. A standalone destination must be independent, have no permanent line, and have no conflicting ordinary execution assignment. Same-factory product/unit positive capability, idle/running availability, non-archived state, and absence of OPEN-transfer participation are required. Normal line machines cannot record as standalone WIP destinations. Remaining Work is operational text, not a new structured routing/capability taxonomy.
- Partial consumption and reprocessing are supported. Same-product/unit consumed quantity must equal Good + Scrap + new Unfinished, enforced with exact PostgreSQL numeric arithmetic. Factory structure/request locks and item/lot row locks revalidate stock/eligibility; retry identity includes WIP inputs, output/note and existing Shift parameters. Negative stock, stale/duplicate consumption and cross-item/factory writes are rejected. Corrections preserve originals/reason/actor/time, adjust created/available stock by the explicit delta, and cannot reduce created stock below already consumed quantity. WIP-input corrections must conserve the immutable consumed amount; V1 does not reverse an existing consumption or move stock to unrelated requests.
- Existing `orders:view` authorizes reads; `orders:edit` plus view authorizes recording, consumption and correction, per person. New tables have scoped read RLS and no authenticated direct-write grants. Readers reuse the existing authenticated permission-checked SECURITY DEFINER architecture with blank search paths; obsolete Recording helpers cannot bypass the new validation. Advisor notices for intentionally exposed authenticated readers are documented, not permission waivers; unrelated existing release-hardening notices were not changed.
- Shift attribution reuses the current server-time/factory-timezone resolver and manual-override audit. TESTING's legitimate user-configured shifts now have windows 07–16 and 16–00; these were preserved, not reverted to the older NULL-window snapshot below. Execution, physical machine states, Planning, `evaluateFlow`, OPEN transfers, capability records, and Production Loss calculations are unchanged by WIP recording.

Selection survives option search/pagination. A compact active-filter summary/Clear all and separate sort remain. Mobile cards use the same filter/query model through one Filters action. Outside click/Escape/native keyboard navigation and EN/AR/RTL are supported; viewport height and narrow form width issues found during verification were corrected.
- Scrap quantity uses effective recorded Scrap. Physical-output Scrap % = Scrap / (Good + Scrap + Unfinished) × 100; zero output is undefined. ## Shift Time Windows V1 — implemented, verified, uncommitted

- Continues the clean `development` checkpoint `a228d99221d9c6401ab676c0cb710f1ae5cb0628`. No commit/push, branch change, or DEV connection. This section supersedes the identity-only Shift V1 behavior described in historical sections below. User/manual acceptance remains the next step.
- Reuses authoritative `factories.timezone`, validated by the existing settings command against PostgreSQL timezone names. TESTING currently uses `Asia/Qatar`; attribution is not hard-coded to Qatar. `clock_timestamp()` is captured once after Recording locks and used both as the entry timestamp and resolver input. PostgreSQL `AT TIME ZONE` supplies factory-local time, including DST. Browser clocks/timezones do not determine shift assignment.
- `production_shifts.start_time` / `end_time` are PostgreSQL time-of-day values. Start is inclusive and end exclusive; an earlier end means overnight. Equal times and incomplete new windows are rejected. Gaps are allowed. A shared private multirange representation supplies matching and the same-factory active-window GiST exclusion constraint, including overnight overlap and concurrent conflicting writes. `btree_gist` is installed in `extensions`; existing factory structure locks remain in place. No business-date column is needed for this identity-only attribution scope.
- One private `resolve_factory_shift` is reused by authenticated `production_shift_context` and Recording submission. Snapshot hydration supplies an initial compact preview, refreshed by a lightweight authenticated read when Recording opens and on existing snapshot refreshes. The preview shows name/window, Overnight where relevant, Auto-detected, factory timezone/time. It is convenience only; submission independently resolves from its persisted server timestamp. A preview from another factory is masked immediately. No active configuration permits transitional NULL; active shifts with no matching window show an explicit no-match and require a deliberate manual selection/reason.
- New Recording retains selected/original shift, automatic shift, requested shift (for stable retries), assignment mode, manual reason, actor and exact server timestamp. Manual selection requires an active same-factory shift and existing `orders:edit` plus `orders:view`; a mismatched/no-match assignment requires a reason of 3–2,000 characters. The UI always requires a reason when manual mode is selected. Existing correction reason/actor/time and previous/new shift ledger are reused. Old callable Recording helpers cannot bypass attribution; quantities, good-only completion, scrap separation, overproduction confirmation, unit validation, locks and idempotency are unchanged. Retries return the original entry before re-resolving, even after schedule edits/archive/boundary changes.
- No historical backfill: old NULL IDs and metadata remain NULL, displayed as Not recorded / غير مسجل. Existing stored IDs remain unchanged by shift-window edits. History still filters/sorts/summarizes persisted IDs; expanded details also show initial manual attribution evidence. Schedule edits use the existing shift audit trigger. Archive prevents new automatic/manual assignment while retaining historical references.
- The two existing user-created shifts named `1` and `2` are preserved exactly, with NULL windows rather than invented schedules. Settings flags them for time configuration. They are not auto-matched; an authorized person can explicitly assign them with a reason. Existing untimed shifts can be archived without fabricating times; creation, editing or reactivation requires a valid window. ## Remaining snapshot performance — measured improvement, timeout acceptance still pending

- Continues the same `development` HEAD `91adac2c763a93043e5024c9cd13cd85c84fdadd`. All pre-existing application, test, locale and migration bytes were checked against the starting SHA-256 inventory and preserved. This task adds one forward migration and `tests/snapshot-contract.test.sql`, and updates this handoff. No application code/design changes, commit/push or DEV connection.
- Local and TESTING were reverified aligned by all **49** migration versions/names before the change. Applied only to TESTING: `20261001063432_build_snapshot_sections_once`. Local and TESTING now have **50**; DEV's carried **11** remains intentionally behind and was not reconnected to. No prior applied migration was edited. No production data cleanup or repair occurred; integration fixtures and diagnostic prototypes rolled back.
- The largest measured costs are JSON construction for **5,000 audit rows (~2.34 MB, 324–360 ms)** and **148 loss-context rows (~5.00 MB, 266–286 ms)**; together they contribute about 97% of the ~7.6 MB payload. The audit timeline index scans about 5,002 rows for the 5,000-row cap, with small incremental sorts and no spill. Loss contexts use the primary-key backward scan. Both plans read from shared buffers. No missing supporting index, large sort, blocking lock or generic/custom parameter-plan instability was demonstrated. No ANALYZE, maintenance operation or permanent settings/timeout change was made.
- Authorization profiling confirms the five previously cached read checks use InitPlans. A complete snapshot still calls the unchanged `has_permission` 278 times (226.5 ms inclusive in a 1,440 ms tracked run); the cached helpers together take about 14 ms. Authorization is material but is not the dominant measured cost. The PostgREST VOLATILE result CTE writes 956 temporary blocks for the large payload; its measured materialization adds about 18 ms, and the observed timeout context remains the function's table-JSON EXECUTE. VOLATILE remains necessary for existing platform/support access audits.
- The migration replaces the UNION of section aggregates followed by `jsonb_object_agg` with a single `jsonb_build_object` containing the same 32 bounded section queries. This avoids the intermediate re-aggregation of already-large JSONB values. All fields, caps, history ordering, metadata, memberships, permissions, truncation flags, SECURITY INVOKER/RLS and READ audit behavior remain intact. Six paired rollback prototypes returned equivalent snapshots and reduced function time by 28–41%; five additional post-application pairs also matched and were faster. Alternative text/JSON and native-array aggregation prototypes were slower and were rejected without persistent changes.
- Ten independent authenticated database calls before: **min 984.759 / median 1,116.128 / max 1,432.550 ms**. Ten after, with a transaction-local **8-second** timeout matching authenticated PostgREST: **min 869.561 / median 1,087.830 / max 1,400.851 ms**, zero SQL timeouts. These independent series show only ~2.5% median improvement; paired improvements must not be presented as a stable end-to-end gain. A PostgREST-shaped EXPLAIN also completed in 1,378 ms after the change.
- Existing signed-in TESTING account: **10 successful manual refresh actions**, configuration navigation and return to Factory Floor, and EN/AR RTL checks preserved LINE 1 Running via alternative, LINE 2 Paused, independent Idle / No Production Demand, current jobs, output and Recording/history. Background polling was also observed. Successful loads clear the warning. However, PostgreSQL logs confirm **one post-migration PostgREST 57014 timeout at 2026-10-01 06:39:23 UTC**, during the regression/profiling window, followed by recovery. The error remains inside `factory_snapshot` at table-JSON assembly. Concurrent diagnostic load is a possible contributor, not a proven cause. **The loading blocker is NOT resolved and is not accepted for checkpointing.** The reason occasional app calls reach 8 seconds while direct calls are much faster remains unestablished.
- New rollback-only contract regression compares the frozen pre-change function with the current function for existing full/limited users, every section field/ID/count, ordered histories, permissions, metadata and truncation; it also checks factory argument isolation, absent factory grants, pending/rejected/no-user behavior, invoker execution and audit volatility. Tracked diff check and both new files pass; the pre-existing extra final blank line at line 72 of applied `20260930125444_production_history_query_consistency.sql` remains untouched. This was a snapshot performance/error-handling defect, not missing permissions or bad execution data.
- Profiling identified repeated `private.has_permission(factory_id,'audit','view')` evaluation across the snapshot's 5,000 audit rows. Remaining repeated checks in role/person grants, status events and loss contexts, plus repeated copying of the growing JSON payload, required `20261001055238_cache_remaining_snapshot_read_authorization`: these four read policies reuse the same authorizer once per factory, and `factory_snapshot` aggregates the same table queries into one object. GET now retains 401 for expired authentication and 403 for real authorization failures; loading failures return HTTP 503 with the existing localized data warning, never internal SQL details. The UI preserves an already-loaded snapshot on transient read failure and clears only its loading warning on recovery, preserving command feedback. A Refresh action is available on that warning.

The shared summary now prioritizes `evaluateFlow` blocked/homeState and buffer verdicts before healthy physical/rerouted presentation. Normal-line summaries and no-demand hints exclude independent members. The component uses that one summary for its existing header chrome. Physical state and OPEN assignment stay separate; a stopped non-blocking spare cannot invent flow blockage. `evaluateFlow`, transfer lifecycle, permanent line IDs, capabilities and execution/Recording/Shift/History/Planning/Loss logic are unchanged.
- Verification: focused Node suites **98/98**; full Node suite **244/244**; TypeScript and production build passed. Audit permission/RLS, Production Execution/assignment/transfer, Production Recording and Shift/History SQL regressions passed with complete rollback. ## Current milestone

- Branch `development`. Production Loss Impact Model V1 and the functional UX audit corrections are the current accepted application state for this checkpoint. The earlier V1 freeze review passed, and the subsequent actual-result correction is included. A successful manual calibration action remains unverified and is a non-blocking acceptance item; do not claim it was tested.
- V1 separates planned operational stops from unplanned problems. Original classification remains on the stop; corrections are audited. Scheduled idle is not production loss. The event model segments Planning demand and captured flow context over time, reuses `evaluateFlow`, open transfers, buffers, product-specific capability rates, and records missing data rather than inventing estimates.
- Engineer profile inputs cover scrap expectation, shutdown/restart scrap, deferability, and recovery capacity. Actuals remain separate. Comparable actuals produce advisory calibration only; engineer approval updates future profiles. A browser can preview V1 impact but submits only factory/event IDs to save. The authenticated TESTING Edge Function recomputes from persisted context using the shared `production-loss-impact.mjs` model and `evaluateFlow`, then a service-role-only RPC writes one immutable reviewed estimate. Authenticated clients cannot execute either estimate writer or insert/update estimate and context tables.
- The existing Downtime Analysis page remains a legacy analysis surface; new classified V1 events are excluded from its old ranking. The final dashboard, Maintenance Requests, Maintenance Gantt, and financial analysis are not implemented.
